The DeepFusion pipeline output in (<strong>t</strong>) better retains saliency and contrast compared to the other methods due to the attention module. Image better viewed in color.</p></figcaption></figure></section></section><section id="sec4dot3-sensors-24-08217"><h3 class="pmc_sec_title">4.3. Performance Analysis</h3>
<p>Most of the published methods analyze the fusion performance of multi-modal data based on metrics designed for single-modal systems. However, conventional metrics often overlook the preservation of original information from input images in the fused output. Ideal image fusion should effectively combine complementary information from both modalities while preserving the integrity of the original data as much as possible. To address this limitation, we propose a keypoint-based analysis defined in Equations (<a href="#FD13-sensors-24-08217" class="usa-link">13</a>)–(<a href="#FD15-sensors-24-08217" class="usa-link">15</a>) to assess the retention of mutually exclusive information from both the input ground truth images in the fused output. Keypoints are particularly suitable for this analysis because they reflect the distribution of original data. However, for completeness of analysis and discussion, we have compared our method with the state-of-the-art networks using the standard visible image matching metrics such as average gradient [<a href="#B62-sensors-24-08217" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B62-sensors-24-08217">62</a>], cross-entropy [<a href="#B107-sensors-24-08217" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B107-sensors-24-08217">107</a>], entropy [<a href="#B108-sensors-24-08217" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B108-sensors-24-08217">108</a>], edge information [<a href="#B109-sensors-24-08217" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B109-sensors-24-08217">109</a>], the Chen–Blum metric [<a href="#B73-sensors-24-08217" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B73-sensors-24-08217">73</a>], the Chen–Varshney metric [<a href="#B74-sensors-24-08217" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B74-sensors-24-08217">74</a>], Qabf [<a href="#B63-sensors-24-08217" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B63-sensors-24-08217">63</a>], mutual information [<a href="#B64-sensors-24-08217" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B64-sensors-24-08217">64</a>], RMSE [<a href="#B72-sensors-24-08217" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B72-sensors-24-08217">72</a>], PSNR [<a href="#B110-sensors-24-08217" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B110-sensors-24-08217">110</a>], SSIM [<a href="#B60-sensors-24-08217" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B60-sensors-24-08217">60</a>] and spatial frequency [<a href="#B65-sensors-24-08217" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B65-sensors-24-08217">65</a>].</p>
<p>In <a href="#sensors-24-08217-f016" class="usa-link">Figure 16</a> and <a href="#sensors-24-08217-f017" class="usa-link">Figure 17</a>, we compare various fusion techniques based on their ability to retain keypoints in the fused output. It is evident from the figure that the existing methods disrupt the original data distribution during fusion, resulting in a reduction in keypoints and the addition of new keypoints that are not present in the input image pairs. In contrast, our proposed method preserves and even enhances keypoints, as demonstrated in <a href="#sensors-24-08217-f014" class="usa-link">Figure 14</a> and reduces outliers as shown in <a href="#sensors-24-08217-f017" class="usa-link">Figure 17</a>e. The common keypoints represented as <em>green</em> in fused images of <a href="#sensors-24-08217-f017" class="usa-link">Figure 17</a>a–d are masked by original keypoints from ground truths (<em>red</em>, <em>blue</em>) for DeepFusion indicating improved fusion performance retaining maximum keypoints from VIS and IR ground truth images. As mentioned earlier while emphasizing keypoint analysis, we also evaluated our method using standard metrics for comprehensive comparison and validation. This approach provides a more nuanced evaluation of image fusion techniques, focusing on the fidelity of retained information crucial for applications requiring accurate preservation of original data characteristics across modalities.</p>
<figure class="fig xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-08217-f016"><h4 class="obj_head">Figure 16.</h4>
<p class="img-box line-height-none margin-x-neg-2 tablet:margin-x-0 text-center"><a class="tileshop" target="_blank" href="https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/core/lw/2.0/html/tileshop_pmc/tileshop_pmc_inline.html?title=Click%20on%20image%20to%20zoom&amp;p=PMC3&amp;id=11679027_sensors-24-08217-g016.jpg"><img class="graphic zoom-in" src="https://cdn.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/blobs/26a2/11679027/0ab6ccd39612/sensors-24-08217-g016.jpg" loading="lazy" height="1060" width="764" alt="Figure 16"></a></p>

It can be observed that no single dataset achieves the best performance across all metrics. <a href="#sensors-24-08217-t002" class="usa-link">Table 2</a> shows the performance comparison of state-of-the-art fusion methods simulated on the Kittler [<a href="#B85-sensors-24-08217" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B85-sensors-24-08217">85</a>] dataset. Every method has unique strengths, which is evident in the variation in top performance metrics across the models. <em>Red</em> color signifies the top performance and <em>Blue</em> shows the second-best performance. For example, the SwinFusion [<a href="#B106-sensors-24-08217" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B106-sensors-24-08217">106</a>] method has the best PSNR performance and SeAFusion [<a href="#B105-sensors-24-08217" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B105-sensors-24-08217">105</a>] method has the best entropy.</p>
<section class="tw xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-08217-t001"><h4 class="obj_head">Table 1.</h4>

<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="table/sensors-24-08217-t002/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div></section><p>This further highlights the need for a universal metric that can quantify the quality of image fusion. In this work, we propose keypoint-based preliminary analysis, which is the first step in this direction. <a href="#sensors-24-08217-t003" class="usa-link">Table 3</a> shows the performance of proposed keypoint metric based on F1 score on the VIFB dataset for the proposed and state-of-the-art networks. The tolerance for matching is fixed at 10 pixels. The keypoint vectors are processed using ‘non-max suppression’ to avoid nearby keypoints dominating the metric, resulting in localized evaluation. In <a href="#sensors-24-08217-t003" class="usa-link">Table 3</a>, it can be observed that the performance of DeepFusion is better than the state-of-the-art methods. We evaluated the metric for multiple datasets and the results obtained are reliably consistent. Since keypoints directly relate the original data with the fused output independent of other parameters, they form the ideal metric for comparing the quality of fusion.</p>
<section class="tw xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-08217-t003"><h4 class="obj_head">Table 3.</h4>
<div class="caption p"><p>Keypoint-based precision (Prec.), recall (Re.) and F1 score (F1) values for various fusion methods on VIFB dataset. The value are derived considering top 100 keypoints in each image with a non-max suppression (NMS) and tolerance window of 10 pixels around the keypoints. The best values of F1 score are shown in <em>Red</em> achieved by the state-of-the-art pipelines. The proposed DeepFusion pipelines achieves the best average output across the dataset with images captured under varied illumination and background conditions.</p></div>

<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="table/sensors-24-08217-t003/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div></section></section></section><section id="sec5-sensors-24-08217"><h2 class="pmc_sec_title">5. Conclusions</h2>
<p>In this work, we addressed the problem of designing a real-time pipeline for multi-modal image fusion for unregistered image pairs. We designed an end-to-end pipeline that takes in multi-modal image pairs and outputs a fused image combining information from both the modalities judiciously to increase the entropy and retain original data in the fused output. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first work in this direction proposing a complete solution. We propose a novel wavelet spectral decomposition-based recursive strip and scale search algorithm to register the image pairs and scale them to a common resolution palette. The registered image pairs are then passed onto the VIRFusionNet module with attention blocks for image fusion. We also propose a novel keypoint metric to quantify the original content retained in the fused image to identify the extent of data corruption. The keypoint metric forms an ideal baseline for comparing various proposed fusion architectures. This is the first metric designed exclusively aiming at multi-modal data, and it reliably outputs consistent performance across multiple datasets and pipeline for efficient fusion analysis. We are currently working towards designing a much more comprehensive network which will try to retain the maximum original information from the raw images in the fused output while improving the information content in the processed output which will successfully support in designing various real-time computer vision and UAV-based applications.</p></section><section id="notes1"><h2 class="pmc_sec_title">Author Contributions</h2>
